Team Blog"}]}</script><script type="application/ld+json" data-next-head="">{"@context":"https://schema.org","@type":"BlogPosting","datePublished":"10/17/2026, 1:00:38 AM","description":"Since October 13, 2026 (Microsoft “Patch Tuesday” release cycle), we have seen some reports of customer issues where Exchange Servers become unstable after a set of October updates are installed.\nSymptoms that can be seen are various, but here are some:\n\nExchange Server OWA may fail with an error MapiExceptionMdbOffline: Unable to make connection to the server. (hr=0x80004005, ec=1142) with event ID 1006 logged in the Application Log.\nExchange email queues might grow during the day, with email crawling to a halt.\nVarious issues related to DAG members not being able to reach quorum have been reported too.\nExchange server reboot might resolve the issues temporarily.\nExchange Server 2016, 2019 or Subscription Edition (SE) might be impacted.\n\nWhat is happening and how to address it\nIssues reported are not related to installation of October 2026 security updates for Exchange Server. This is not a “known issue” related to October 2026 Exchange update. Any version of Exchange that runs on impacted versions of Windows could be impacted.\nThe following Windows Server updates can cause above mentioned Exchange Server failures after certain time:\n\nKB5126646 (Windows Server 2025)\nKB5126655 (Windows Server 2022)\nKB5126651 (Windows Server 2019)\nKB5126868 (Windows Server 2016)\n\nNot all Exchange servers will be impacted by installation of those updates equally; the severity and speed of impact varies based on Exchange server load. Some servers might never see a problem.\nOur recommendation\n\nDo not uninstall October 2026 Windows Server updates.\nFrom an elevated Command Prompt on an Exchange Server, run:\n\nreg add &quot;HKLM\\SOFTWARE\\Microsoft\\Rpc&quot; /v EpmapSavedContextCap /t REG_DWORD /d 65536 /f\n\nReboot the server.\nAfter the reboot, monitor the environment and confirm whether the issue recurs. If it re-occurs, raise the limit. This has been shown to solve the problem based on customer reported cases that we have assisted to date.\n\nMore information can be found in Users might be unable to sign in to OWA or send emails on Exchange Server (Windows Server known issues and notifications).\nThe Exchange servers may become unstable after installation of October 2026 Windows document.getElementById('social-share-footer'); if (footerContainer) { var currentURL = window.location.href; var strippedURL = currentURL.split('?')[0]; var currentTitle = document.title; var encodedURL = encodeURIComponent(strippedURL); var encodedTitle = encodeURIComponent(currentTitle); var socialLinks = footerContainer.querySelectorAll('.social-link-sharing'); // Encode page URL and title values in aka.ms share hrefs. // https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/exchange/exchange-servers-may-become-unstable-after-installation-of-october-2026-windows-/4565382 / BlogMessagePage are rendered server-side but // Handlebars does not URL-encode, so a title with `\u0026`, `#`, // or spaces would produce a malformed query string. Here we // find the raw values in each href and swap them for URL- // encoded ones. Old `page.url` / `page-name` placeholders // are still replaced as a defensive fallback. socialLinks.forEach(function(link) { var currentHref = link.getAttribute('href') || ''; var newHref = currentHref; if (strippedURL) { newHref = newHref.split(strippedURL).join(encodedURL); } if (currentTitle) { newHref = newHref.split(currentTitle).join(encodedTitle); } newHref = newHref.replace('page.url', encodedURL).replace('page-name', encodedTitle); if (newHref !== currentHref) { link.setAttribute('href', newHref); } }); // Update RSS links for Blogs/Events pages. // Normalize the path segment (lower-case, drop leading slash, // take the first segment only) so `/Blogs`, `/blogs`, `/BLOGS/`, // and `/blogs/foo` all resolve to the same lower-case mapping key. var urlPath = new URL(currentURL).pathname; var rssLink = footerContainer.querySelector('.social-share-rss-footer'); if (rssLink \u0026\u0026 (/\\/blogs/i.test(urlPath) || /\\/events/i.test(urlPath))) { var pathSegment = urlPath.replace(/^\\//, '').toLowerCase().split('/')[0]; var styleMapping = { blogs: 'blog', events: 'occasion' }; var rssStyle = styleMapping[pathSegment]; if (rssStyle) { rssLink.setAttribute('href', 'https://aka.ms/MTC/RSS-Community-Footer?interaction.style=' + rssStyle + '\u0026feeds.replies=false' ); } } } \u003c/script\u003e This inline handler exists so screen readers still get an accurate expanded/collapsed announcement and Esc still collapses the rail even in the rare case the pagescript fails to load. When the pagescript is loaded, these DOM writes are idempotent \\u2014 both scripts converge on the same aria-expanded value. */ (function () { /* aria-expanded sync via CONTAINER-level listeners only (never on the button itself). Container listeners match the CSS :hover / :focus-within triggers that already control panel visibility, so state stays in sync without interfering with the Esc handler below (button-level focus/blur was previously observed to break Esc). */ var rails = document.querySelectorAll('[data-mtc-share-rail]'); rails.forEach(function (rail) { var btn = rail.querySelector('button'); if (!btn) return; var open = function () { btn.setAttribute('aria-expanded', 'true'); }; var close = function () { var hovered = false; try { hovered = rail.matches(':hover'); } catch (err) { /* no-op */ } if (!hovered \u0026\u0026 !rail.contains(document.activeElement)) { btn.setAttribute('aria-expanded', 'false'); } }; rail.addEventListener('mouseenter', open); rail.addEventListener('focusin', open); rail.addEventListener('mouseleave', close); rail.addEventListener('focusout', function () { setTimeout(close, 0); }); }); document.addEventListener('keydown', function (e) { if (e.key !== 'Escape' \u0026\u0026 e.key !== 'Esc') return; var rails = document.querySelectorAll('[data-mtc-share-rail]'); rails.forEach(function (rail) { var isOpen = false; try { isOpen = rail.matches(':hover') || rail.matches(':focus-within'); } catch (err) { /* no-op */ } if (!isOpen \u0026\u0026 rail.getAttribute('data-expanded') !== 'true') return; rail.setAttribute('data-esc-closed', 'true'); rail.removeAttribute('data-expanded'); var focused = document.activeElement; if (focused \u0026\u0026 rail.contains(focused)) { try { focused.blur(); } catch (err) { /* no-op */ } } var clearEsc = function () { rail.removeAttribute('data-esc-closed'); rail.removeEventListener('mouseleave', clearEsc); rail.removeEventListener('focusin', clearEsc); }; rail.addEventListener('mouseleave', clearEsc); rail.addEventListener('focusin', clearEsc); }); }); })(); \u003c/script\u003e rgs":[]}},"canFreezeTkbArticle":{"__typename":"PolicyResult","failureReason":{"__typename":"FailureReason","message":"error.lithium.policies.tkb.action_can_freeze_article.accessDenied","key":"error.lithium.policies.tkb.action_can_freeze_article.accessDenied","args":[]}},"canReportAbuse":{"__typename":"PolicyResult","failureReason":null},"canDeleteDraft":{"__typename":"PolicyResult","failureReason":{"__typename":"FailureReason","message":"error.lithium.policies.delete_drafts.accessDenied","key":"error.lithium.policies.delete_drafts.accessDenied","args":[]}}},"contentWorkflow":{"__typename":"ContentWorkflow","state":"PUBLISH","scheduledPublishTime":null,"scheduledTimezone":null,"userContext":{"__typename":"MessageWorkflowContext","canSubmitForReview":null,"canEdit":false,"canRecall":null,"canSubmitForPublication":null,"canReturnToAuthor":null,"canPublish":null,"canReturnToReview":null,"canSchedule":false},"shortScheduledTimezone":null},"readOnly":false,"editFrozen":false,"showMoveIndicator":false,"moderationData":{"__ref":"ModerationData:moderation_data:4565382"},"teaser":"\u003cP\u003eMore information related to issues that some Exchange servers might experience after installation of October 2026 Windows updates.\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cspan class=\"lia-media-object lia-media-is-center lia-media-size-default\" data-image-alt=\"\"\u003e\u003cbutton class=\"lia-media-unstyled-btn\" type=\"button\" aria-haspopup=\"true\" aria-label=\"Enlarge Image\"\u003e\u003cimg src=\"https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/t5/s/gxcuf89792/images/bS00NTY1MzgyLTZTSlF6Ng?image-dimensions=370x214\u0026amp;revision=3\" width=\"370\" height=\"214\" alt=\"\" /\u003e\u003c/button\u003e\u003c/span\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u0026nbsp;\u003c/P\u003e","body":"\u003cP\u003eSince October 13, 2026 (Microsoft “Patch Tuesday” release cycle), we have seen some reports of customer issues where Exchange Servers become unstable after a set of October updates are installed.\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eSymptoms that can be seen are various, but here are some:\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cUL\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003eExchange Server OWA may fail with an error MapiExceptionMdbOffline: Unable to make connection to the server. (hr=0x80004005, ec=1142) with event ID 1006 logged in the Application Log.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003eExchange email queues might grow during the day, with email crawling to a halt.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003eVarious issues related to DAG members not being able to reach quorum have been reported too.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003eExchange server reboot might resolve the issues temporarily.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003eExchange Server 2016, 2019 or Subscription Edition (SE) might be impacted.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003c/UL\u003e\n\u003cH1 id=\"community-4565382-toc-hId--1131761460\"\u003eWhat is happening and how to address it\u003c/H1\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eIssues reported are \u003cEM\u003enot\u003c/EM\u003e related to installation of \u003cA href=\"https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/exchange/released-october-2026-exchange-server-security-updates/4563014\" target=\"_blank\"\u003eOctober 2026 security updates for Exchange Server\u003c/A\u003e. This is not a “known issue” related to October 2026 Exchange update. Any version of Exchange that runs on impacted versions of Windows could be impacted.\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eThe following Windows Server updates can cause above mentioned Exchange Server failures after certain time:\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cUL\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003e\u003cA href=\"https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/servicing/os/windows-server/2026/10/kb5126646-windows-server-2025-security-update\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noopener noreferrer\"\u003eKB5126646\u003c/A\u003e (Windows Server 2025)\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003e\u003cA style=\"font-style: normal; font-weight: 400; background-color: rgb(255, 255, 255);\" href=\"https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/servicing/os/windows-server/2026/10/kb5126655-windows-server-2022-security-update\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noopener noreferrer\"\u003eKB5126655\u003c/A\u003e\u003cSPAN style=\"color: rgb(30, 30, 30);\"\u003e (Windows Server 2022)\u003c/SPAN\u003e\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003e\u003cA style=\"font-style: normal; font-weight: 400; background-color: rgb(255, 255, 255);\" href=\"https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/servicing/os/windows-10/2026/10/kb5126651-windows-10-1809-security-update\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noopener noreferrer\"\u003eKB5126651\u003c/A\u003e\u003cSPAN style=\"color: rgb(30, 30, 30);\"\u003e (Windows Server 2019)\u003c/SPAN\u003e\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003e\u003cA style=\"font-style: normal; font-weight: 400; background-color: rgb(255, 255, 255);\" href=\"https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/servicing/os/windows-10/2026/10/kb5126868-windows-10-1607-security-update\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noopener noreferrer\"\u003eKB5126868\u003c/A\u003e\u003cSPAN style=\"color: rgb(30, 30, 30);\"\u003e (Windows Server 2016)\u003c/SPAN\u003e\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003c/UL\u003e\n\u003cP style=\"background: #FFFF99; padding: .5em; margin: 1em 0 1em 0;\"\u003eNot all Exchange servers will be impacted by installation of those updates equally; the severity and speed of impact varies based on Exchange server load. Some servers might never see a problem.\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cH3 id=\"community-4565382-toc-hId-2056815951\"\u003eOur recommendation\u003c/H3\u003e\n\u003cOL\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eDo not uninstall\u003c/STRONG\u003e October 2026 Windows Server updates.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003eFrom an elevated Command Prompt on an Exchange Server, run:\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003c/OL\u003e\n\u003cPRE class=\"lia-indent-padding-left-30px\" style=\"padding-left : 30px;\"\u003ereg add \"HKLM\\SOFTWARE\\Microsoft\\Rpc\" /v EpmapSavedContextCap /t REG_DWORD /d 65536 /f\u003c/PRE\u003e\n\u003cOL start=\"3\"\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003eReboot the server.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003eAfter the reboot, monitor the environment and confirm whether the issue recurs. If it re-occurs, raise the limit. This has been shown to solve the problem based on customer reported cases that we have assisted to date.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003c/OL\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eMore information can be found in \u003cA href=\"https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/release-health/status-windows-server-2025#5047msgdesc\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noopener noreferrer\"\u003eUsers might be unable to sign in to OWA or send emails on Exchange Server\u003c/A\u003e (Windows Server known issues and notifications).\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cSPAN class=\"lia-text-color-12\"\u003eThe Exchange Team\u003c/SPAN\u003e\u003c/P\u003e","body@stringLength":"3997","rawBody":"\u003cP\u003eSince October 13, 2026 (Microsoft “Patch Tuesday” release cycle), we have seen some reports of customer issues where Exchange Servers become unstable after a set of October updates are installed.\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eSymptoms that can be seen are various, but here are some:\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cUL\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003eExchange Server OWA may fail with an error MapiExceptionMdbOffline: Unable to make connection to the server. (hr=0x80004005, ec=1142) with event ID 1006 logged in the Application Log.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003eExchange email queues might grow during the day, with email crawling to a halt.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003eVarious issues related to DAG members not being able to reach quorum have been reported too.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003eExchange server reboot might resolve the issues temporarily.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003eExchange Server 2016, 2019 or Subscription Edition (SE) might be impacted.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003c/UL\u003e\n\u003cH1\u003eWhat is happening and how to address it\u003c/H1\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eIssues reported are \u003cEM\u003enot\u003c/EM\u003e related to installation of \u003cA href=\"https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/blog/exchange/released-october-2026-exchange-server-security-updates/4563014\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noopener\"\u003eOctober 2026 security updates for Exchange Server\u003c/A\u003e. This is not a “known issue” related to October 2026 Exchange update. Any version of Exchange that runs on impacted versions of Windows could be impacted.\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eThe following Windows Server updates can cause above mentioned Exchange Server failures after certain time:\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cUL\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003e\u003cA href=\"https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/servicing/os/windows-server/2026/10/kb5126646-windows-server-2025-security-update\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noopener\"\u003eKB5126646\u003c/A\u003e (Windows Server 2025)\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003e\u003cA style=\"font-style: normal; font-weight: 400; background-color: rgb(255, 255, 255);\" href=\"https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/servicing/os/windows-server/2026/10/kb5126655-windows-server-2022-security-update\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noopener\"\u003eKB5126655\u003c/A\u003e\u003cSPAN style=\"color: rgb(30, 30, 30);\"\u003e (Windows Server 2022)\u003c/SPAN\u003e\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003e\u003cA style=\"font-style: normal; font-weight: 400; background-color: rgb(255, 255, 255);\" href=\"https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/servicing/os/windows-10/2026/10/kb5126651-windows-10-1809-security-update\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noopener\"\u003eKB5126651\u003c/A\u003e\u003cSPAN style=\"color: rgb(30, 30, 30);\"\u003e (Windows Server 2019)\u003c/SPAN\u003e\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003e\u003cA style=\"font-style: normal; font-weight: 400; background-color: rgb(255, 255, 255);\" href=\"https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/servicing/os/windows-10/2026/10/kb5126868-windows-10-1607-security-update\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noopener\"\u003eKB5126868\u003c/A\u003e\u003cSPAN style=\"color: rgb(30, 30, 30);\"\u003e (Windows Server 2016)\u003c/SPAN\u003e\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003c/UL\u003e\n\u003cP style=\"background: #FFFF99; padding: .5em; margin: 1em 0 1em 0;\"\u003eNot all Exchange servers will be impacted by installation of those updates equally; the severity and speed of impact varies based on Exchange server load. Some servers might never see a problem.\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cH3\u003eOur recommendation\u003c/H3\u003e\n\u003cOL\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003e\u003cSTRONG\u003eDo not uninstall\u003c/STRONG\u003e October 2026 Windows Server updates.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003eFrom an elevated Command Prompt on an Exchange Server, run:\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003c/OL\u003e\n\u003cPRE class=\"lia-indent-padding-left-30px\"\u003ereg add \"HKLM\\SOFTWARE\\Microsoft\\Rpc\" /v EpmapSavedContextCap /t REG_DWORD /d 65536 /f\u003c/PRE\u003e\n\u003cOL start=\"3\"\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003eReboot the server.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003cLI\u003eAfter the reboot, monitor the environment and confirm whether the issue recurs. If it re-occurs, raise the limit. This has been shown to solve the problem based on customer reported cases that we have assisted to date.\u003c/LI\u003e\n\u003c/OL\u003e\n\u003cP\u003eMore information can be found in \u003cA href=\"https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/release-health/status-windows-server-2025#5047msgdesc\" target=\"_blank\" rel=\"noopener\"\u003eUsers might be unable to sign in to OWA or send emails on Exchange Server\u003c/A\u003e (Windows Server known issues and notifications).\u003c/P\u003e\n\u003cP\u003e\u003cSPAN class=\"lia-text-color-12\"\u003eThe Exchange ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n   Sidebar share rail\r\n   ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n   Uses structural selectors (`.social-share \u003e button`, `\u003e div`, `\u003e a`) so\r\n   inner styles land regardless of CSS Modules class hashing. Panel reveal\r\n   uses `!important` to override Bootstrap's global `[hidden]` rule. */\r\n\r\n.social-share {\r\n  position: fixed;\r\n  top: 50%;\r\n  left: 4px;\r\n  transform: translateY(-50%);\r\n  z-index: 1000;\r\n  width: 280px;\r\n  height: 36px;\r\n  pointer-events: none;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/* Toggle button (first direct \u003cbutton\u003e child) */\r\n.social-share \u003e button {\r\n  position: absolute;\r\n  top: 0;\r\n  left: 0;\r\n  z-index: 1;\r\n  display: flex;\r\n  align-items: center;\r\n  justify-content: center;\r\n  width: 36px;\r\n  height: 36px;\r\n  border: none;\r\n  border-radius: 10px;\r\n  background: var(--lia-bs-body-bg, #ffffff);\r\n  box-shadow:\r\n    0 2px 6px rgba(var(--lia-bs-black-rgb, 0, 0, 0), 0.08),\r\n    0 8px 20px rgba(var(--lia-bs-black-rgb, 0, 0, 0), 0.08);\r\n  cursor: pointer;\r\n  color: var(--lia-bs-body-color, #242424);\r\n  pointer-events: auto;\r\n  transition:\r\n    opacity 0.15s ease,\r\n    background-color 0.2s ease,\r\n    box-shadow 0.2s ease;\r\n}\r\n\r\n.social-share \u003e button:hover {\r\n  background: var(--lia-bs-gray-100, #f5f5f5);\r\n  box-shadow:\r\n    0 4px 10px rgba(var(--lia-bs-black-rgb, 0, 0, 0), 0.1),\r\n    0 12px 28px rgba(var(--lia-bs-black-rgb, 0, 0, 0), 0.1);\r\n}\r\n\r\n.social-share \u003e button:focus-visible {\r\n  /* #5b5fc7 is a brand social color (per exception list) — not tokenized */\r\n  outline: 2px solid #5b5fc7;\r\n  outline-offset: 2px;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/* Panel (direct \u003cdiv\u003e child of container — only div child in the DOM) */\r\n.social-share \u003e div {\r\n  position: absolute;\r\n  /* Vertically centre the panel around the button's position so it doesn't\r\n     get cut off at the bottom of the viewport when the rail is near mid-height. */\r\n  top: 50%;\r\n  left: 0;\r\n  transform: translateY(-50%);\r\n  z-index: 2;\r\n  display: none;\r\n  flex-direction: column;\r\n  background: var(--lia-bs-body-bg, #ffffff);\r\n  border-radius: 16px;\r\n  box-shadow:\r\n    0 4px 10px rgba(var(--lia-bs-black-rgb, 0, 0, 0), 0.06),\r\n    0 16px 40px rgba(var(--lia-bs-black-rgb, 0, 0, 0), 0.12);\r\n  padding: 8px;\r\n  min-width: 220px;\r\n  gap: 0;\r\n  pointer-events: auto;\r\n  /* Cap height to the viewport and scroll if still too tall (small screens). */\r\n  max-height: calc(100vh - 32px);\r\n  overflow-y: auto;\r\n  overscroll-behavior: contain;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/* Reveal panel on hover / focus / click-toggle — !important to override\r\n   Bootstrap's global [hidden] { display: none !important; }.\r\n   `[data-expanded=\"true\"]` is set by the inline script when the user clicks\r\n   or taps the button (mobile / non-hover interactions). */\r\n.social-share:hover \u003e div,\r\n.social-share:focus-within \u003e div,\r\n.social-share[data-expanded='true'] \u003e div {\r\n  display: flex !important;\r\n}\r\n\r\n.social-share:hover \u003e button,\r\n.social-share:focus-within \u003e button,\r\n.social-share[data-expanded='true'] \u003e button {\r\n  opacity: 0;\r\n  pointer-events: none;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/* Esc-close override: after Esc, the JS adds `data-esc-closed=\"true\"` so\r\n   even a hovering mouse cannot re-open the panel. Cleared on mouseleave. */\r\n.social-share[data-esc-closed='true'] \u003e div {\r\n  display: none !important;\r\n}\r\n\r\n.social-share[data-esc-closed='true'] \u003e button {\r\n  opacity: 1;\r\n  pointer-events: auto;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/* Panel internals — heading (first \u003cspan\u003e child of panel) */\r\n.social-share \u003e div \u003e span {\r\n  display: flex;\r\n  align-items: center;\r\n  gap: 14px;\r\n  padding: 10px 12px 12px;\r\n  font-size: 15px;\r\n  font-weight: 600;\r\n  color: var(--lia-bs-body-color, #242424);\r\n  line-height: 1.2;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/* Every share row (each \u003ca\u003e child of panel) */\r\n.social-share \u003e div \u003e a {\r\n  display: flex;\r\n  align-items: center;\r\n  gap: 14px;\r\n  padding: 10px 12px;\r\n  border-radius: 8px;\r\n  border: 2px solid transparent;\r\n  text-decoration: none;\r\n  color: var(--lia-bs-body-color, #242424);\r\n  font-size: 14px;\r\n  line-height: 1.2;\r\n  transition:\r\n    background-color 0.15s ease,\r\n    border-color 0.15s ease;\r\n}\r\n\r\n.social-share \u003e div \u003e a:hover {\r\n  /* #f0f0fb / #5b5fc7 are brand social colors (per exception list) — not tokenized */\r\n  background: #f0f0fb;\r\n  border-color: #5b5fc7;\r\n  box-shadow: 0 0 0 1px #5b5fc7 inset;\r\n  text-decoration: none;\r\n  color: var(--lia-bs-body-color, #242424);\r\n}\r\n\r\n.social-share \u003e div \u003e a:focus {\r\n  outline: none;\r\n}\r\n\r\n.social-share \u003e div \u003e a:focus-visible {\r\n  /* brand social colors (per exception list) — not tokenized */\r\n  outline: none;\r\n  border-color: #5b5fc7;\r\n  background: #f0f0fb;\r\n  box-shadow: 0 0 0 1px #5b5fc7 inset;\r\n}\r\n\r\n/* Icon slot (first \u003cspan\u003e inside heading or link) */\r\n.social-share \u003e div \u003e span \u003e @media only screen and (max-width: 1083px) {\r\n    display: flex;\r\n    flex-wrap: wrap;\r\n    padding: 1.875rem 1.5rem 1rem;\r\n  }\r\n}\r\n/* ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n   Sidebar share rail\r\n   ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\r\n   Uses structural selectors (`.social-share \u003e button`, `\u003e div`, `\u003e a`) so\r\n   inner styles land regardless of CSS Modules class hashing. Panel reveal\r\n   uses `!important` to override Bootstrap's global `[hidden]` rule. */\r\n.custom_widget_MicrosoftFooter_social-share_dzx6r_287 {\r\n  position: fixed;\r\n  top: 50%;\r\n  left: 0.25rem;\r\n  transform: translateY(-50%);\r\n  z-index: 1000;\r\n  width: 17.5rem;\r\n  height: 2.25rem;\r\n  pointer-events: none;\r\n}\r\n/* Toggle button (first direct \u003cbutton\u003e child) */\r\n.custom_widget_MicrosoftFooter_social-share_dzx6r_287 \u003e button {\r\n  position: absolute;\r\n  top: 0;\r\n  left: 0;\r\n  z-index: 1;\r\n  display: flex;\r\n  align-items: center;\r\n  justify-content: center;\r\n  width: 2.25rem;\r\n  height: 2.25rem;\r\n  border: none;\r\n  border-radius: 0.625rem;\r\n  background: var(--lia-bs-body-bg, #ffffff);\r\n  box-shadow:\r\n    0 2px 6px rgba(var(--lia-bs-black-rgb, 0, 0, 0), 0.08),\r\n    0 8px 20px rgba(var(--lia-bs-black-rgb, 0, 0, 0), 0.08);\r\n  cursor: pointer;\r\n  color: var(--lia-bs-body-color, #242424);\r\n  pointer-events: auto;\r\n  transition:\r\n    opacity 0.15s ease,\r\n    background-color 0.2s ease,\r\n    box-shadow 0.2s ease;\r\n}\r\n.custom_widget_MicrosoftFooter_social-share_dzx6r_287 \u003e button:hover {\r\n  background: var(--lia-bs-gray-100, #f5f5f5);\r\n  box-shadow:\r\n    0 4px 10px rgba(var(--lia-bs-black-rgb, 0, 0, 0), 0.1),\r\n    0 12px 28px rgba(var(--lia-bs-black-rgb, 0, 0, 0), 0.1);\r\n}\r\n.custom_widget_MicrosoftFooter_social-share_dzx6r_287 \u003e button:focus-visible {\r\n  /* #5b5fc7 is a brand social color (per exception list) — not tokenized */\r\n  outline: 2px solid #5b5fc7;\r\n  outline-offset: 2px;\r\n}\r\n/* Panel (direct \u003cdiv\u003e child of container — only div child in the DOM) */\r\n.custom_widget_MicrosoftFooter_social-share_dzx6r_287 \u003e div {\r\n  position: absolute;\r\n  /* Vertically centre the panel around the button's position so it doesn't\r\n     get cut off at the bottom of the viewport when the rail is near mid-height. */\r\n  top: 50%;\r\n  left: 0;\r\n  transform: translateY(-50%);\r\n  z-index: 2;\r\n  display: none;\r\n  flex-direction: column;\r\n  background: var(--lia-bs-body-bg, #ffffff);\r\n  border-radius: 1rem;\r\n  box-shadow:\r\n    0 4px 10px rgba(var(--lia-bs-black-rgb, 0, 0, 0), 0.06),\r\n    0 16px 40px rgba(var(--lia-bs-black-rgb, 0, 0, 0), 0.12);\r\n  padding: 0.5rem;\r\n  min-width: 13.75rem;\r\n  gap: 0;\r\n  pointer-events: auto;\r\n  /* Cap height to the viewport and scroll if still too tall (small screens). */\r\n  max-height: calc(100vh - 2rem);\r\n  overflow-y: auto;\r\n  overscroll-behavior: contain;\r\n}\r\n/* Reveal panel on hover / focus / click-toggle — !important to override\r\n   Bootstrap's global [hidden] { display: none !important; }.\r\n   `[data-expanded=\"true\"]` is set by the inline script when the user clicks\r\n   or taps the button (mobile / non-hover interactions). */\r\n.custom_widget_MicrosoftFooter_social-share_dzx6r_287:hover \u003e div,\r\n.custom_widget_MicrosoftFooter_social-share_dzx6r_287:focus-within \u003e div,\r\n.custom_widget_MicrosoftFooter_social-share_dzx6r_287[data-expanded='true'] \u003e div {\r\n  display: flex !important;\r\n}\r\n.custom_widget_MicrosoftFooter_social-share_dzx6r_287:hover \u003e button,\r\n.custom_widget_MicrosoftFooter_social-share_dzx6r_287:focus-within \u003e button,\r\n.custom_widget_MicrosoftFooter_social-share_dzx6r_287[data-expanded='true'] \u003e button {\r\n  opacity: 0;\r\n  pointer-events: none;\r\n}\r\n/* Esc-close override: after Esc, the JS adds `data-esc-closed=\"true\"` so\r\n   even a hovering mouse cannot re-open the panel. Cleared on mouseleave. */\r\n.custom_widget_MicrosoftFooter_social-share_dzx6r_287[data-esc-closed='true'] \u003e div {\r\n  display: none !important;\r\n}\r\n.custom_widget_MicrosoftFooter_social-share_dzx6r_287[data-esc-closed='true'] \u003e button {\r\n  opacity: 1;\r\n  pointer-events: auto;\r\n}\r\n/* Panel internals — heading (first \u003cspan\u003e child of panel) */\r\n.custom_widget_MicrosoftFooter_social-share_dzx6r_287 \u003e div \u003e span {\r\n  display: flex;\r\n  align-items: center;\r\n  gap: 0.875rem;\r\n  padding: 0.625rem 0.75rem 0.75rem;\r\n  font-size: 0.9375rem;\r\n  font-weight: 600;\r\n  color: var(--lia-bs-body-color, #242424);\r\n  line-height: 1.2;\r\n}\r\n/* Every share row (each \u003ca\u003e child of panel) */\r\n.custom_widget_MicrosoftFooter_social-share_dzx6r_287 \u003e div \u003e a {\r\n  display: flex;\r\n  align-items: center;\r\n  gap: 0.875rem;\r\n  padding: 0.625rem 0.75rem;\r\n  border-radius: 0.5rem;\r\n  border: 2px solid transparent;\r\n  text-decoration: none;\r\n  color: var(--lia-bs-body-color, #242424);\r\n  font-size: 0.875rem;\r\n  line-height: 1.2;\r\n  transition:\r\n    background-color 0.15s ease,\r\n    border-color 0.15s ease;\r\n}\r\n.custom_widget_MicrosoftFooter_social-share_dzx6r_287 \u003e div \u003e a:hover {\r\n  /* #f0f0fb / #5b5fc7 are brand social colors (per exception list) — not tokenized */\r\n  background: #f0f0fb;\r\n  border-color: #5b5fc7;\r\n  box-shadow: 0 0 0 1px #5b5fc7 inset;\r\n  text-decoration: none;\r\n  color: var(--lia-bs-body-color, #242424);\r\n}\r\n.custom_widget_MicrosoftFooter_social-share_dzx6r_287 \u003e div \u003e a:focus {\r\n  outline: none;\r\n}\r\n.custom_widget_MicrosoftFooter_social-share_dzx6r_287 \u003e div \u003e a:focus-visible {\r\n  /* brand social colors (per exception list) — not tokenized */\r\n  outline: none;\r\n  border-color: #5b5fc7;\r\n  background: #f0f0fb;\r\n  box-shadow: 0 0 0 1px #5b5fc7 inset;\r\n}\r\n/* Icon slot (first \u003cspan\u003e inside heading or link) Exchange servers may become unstable after installation of October 2026 Windows updates","moderationData":{"__ref":"ModerationData:moderation_data:4565665"},"body":"\u003cP\u003ea { text-decoration: none; color: #464feb; } tr th, tr td { border: 1px solid #e6e6e6; } tr th { background-color: #f5f5f5; }\u003c/P\u003e\u003cP\u003eI have not installed the October 2026 updates, but I am experiencing some similar issues.\u003c/P\u003e\u003cP\u003eUsers are seeing abnormal or incomplete mailbox search results, and local mailbox moves are very slow. After restarting the Exchange servers, the issues temporarily improve for a few hours. Interestingly, after moving a user's mailbox to another database, mailbox search starts working normally again for that user.\u003c/P\u003e\u003cP\u003eIn my environment, these issues started after installing the \u003cSTRONG\u003eSeptember 2026 V2 Exchange Server Security Updates\u003c/STRONG\u003e. I have modified the relevant registry value and am currently validating whether this resolves the Exchange servers may become unstable after installation of October 2026 Windows updates","moderationData":{"__ref":"ModerationData:moderation_data:4565527"},"body":"\u003cP\u003eWe are seeing the same issue on Exchange Server 2016 (15.1.2507.6) running on Windows Server 2016 after installing KB5126868. After a reboot, the server operates normally for approximately 18–24 hours, then becomes unstable again. During the degraded state, the server itself remains remotely responsive (WinRM, RPC, SMB and HTTPS are reachable), but RDP login stops working normally. Microsoft Exchange Diagnostics enters a continuous crash/restart loop. During the latest incident, the service terminated unexpectedly more than 200 times. We also observed unexpected terminations of Exchange Mailbox Replication, IMAP4 and IMAP4 Backend services. System resources do not appear to be exhausted: approximately 34 GB of 64 GB RAM was still available during the issue, and commit usage was only ~49%. A reboot temporarily restores normal operation, but the issue returns within approximately one day. We have not applied the EpmapSavedContextCap workaround yet. Is there a permanent fix planned for this issue? Also, is uninstalling KB5126868 currently considered a supported temporary mitigation for affected Exchange Server 2016 systems?\u003c/P\u003e","body@stripHtml({\"removeProcessingText\":false,\"removeSpoilerMarkup\":false,\"removeTocMarkup\":false,\"truncateLength\":200})@stringLength":"203","kudosSumWeight":0,"reactionCounts":[],"postTime":"2026-10-17T09:19:15.387-07:00","lastPublishTime":"2026-10-17T09:19:15.387-07:00","metrics":{"__typename":"MessageMetrics","views":3489},"visibilityScope":"PUBLIC","aiProcessingStatus":"DISABLED","aiModerationResult":null,"latestAppeal":null,"placeholder":false,"showMoveIndicator":false,"originalMessageForPlaceholder":null,"messagePolicies":{"__typename":"MessagePolicies","canModerateSpamMessage":{"__typename":"PolicyResult","failureReason":{"__typename":"FailureReason","message":"error.lithium.policies.feature.moderation_spam.action.moderate_entity.allowed.accessDenied","key":"error.lithium.policies.feature.moderation_spam.action.moderate_entity.allowed.accessDenied","args":[]}},"canModerateEntity":{"__typename":"PolicyResult","failureReason":{"__typename":"FailureReason","message":"error.lithium.policies.moderation.action.moderatableentity.moderate_entity.allow.accessDenied","key":"error.lithium.policies.moderation.action.moderatableentity.moderate_entity.allow.accessDenied","args":[]}},"canEdit":{"__typename":"PolicyResult","failureReason":{"__typename":"FailureReason","message":"error.lithium.policies.forums.action_allow.edit_message.accessDenied","key":"error.lithium.policies.forums.action_allow.edit_message.accessDenied","args":[]}},"canKudo":{"__typename":"PolicyResult","failureReason":{"__typename":"FailureReason","message":"error.lithium.policies.kudos.action.entity.give_kudos.allow.accessDenied","key":"error.lithium.policies.kudos.action.entity.give_kudos.allow.accessDenied","args":[]}},"canTag":{"__typename":"PolicyResult","failureReason":{"__typename":"FailureReason","message":"error.lithium.policies.labels.action.labelableentity.set_labels.allow.accessDenied","key":"error.lithium.policies.labels.action.labelableentity.set_labels.allow Exchange servers may become unstable after installation of October 2026 Windows updates","moderationData":{"__ref":"ModerationData:moderation_data:4565555"},"body":"\u003cP\u003eSame, were on exchange on prem SE latest SU, OS 2019. Didn't have this issue until oct 2026 updates. All kinds of crazy RPC/MAPI issues in our DAG, but rebooting (ALL) the servers clears it up.\u003c/P\u003e\u003cP\u003eAll sorts of weird MAPI/RPC errors. DAG DB's failing and cant even fail over. Rebooting all the exchange servers seems to resolve the issue. Not a very good solution every 18-24 Exchange servers may become unstable after installation of October 2026 Windows updates","moderationData":{"__ref":"ModerationData:moderation_data:4565458"},"body":"\u003cP\u003eit is listed here:\u003cBR /\u003ehttps://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/release-health/status-windows-server-2025#5047msgdesc\u003cBR /\u003e\u003cBR /\u003eThe October 2026 update introduced a default limit on the amount of memory used by Endpoint Mapper when handling Endpoint Lookup calls. For workloads such as Exchange Server, the default limit of 512 simultaneous Endpoint Lookup calls might not be sufficient under heavy load. Administrators can use this registry setting to increase the maximum number of simultaneous Endpoint Lookup calls, which increases the amount of memory that Endpoint Mapper can
